I've uploaded a python code repository in the directory sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2. Consider the following issue description:

<issue_description>
New Rule: In ON clause of JOIN statement specify source table first and join table second
<!--Note: This is for general enhancements to the project. Please use the Bug report template instead to raise parsing/linting/syntax issues for existing supported dialects-->
From GitLab SQL style guide: https://about.gitlab.com/handbook/business-technology/data-team/platform/sql-style-guide/#joins

`Specify the order of a join with the FROM table first and JOIN table second:`
```sql
  -- Good
  FROM source
  LEFT JOIN other_source
    ON source.id = other_source.id
  WHERE ...

  -- Bad
  FROM source
  LEFT JOIN other_source
    ON other_source.id = source.id
  WHERE ...
```

More generally put the SOURCE table first and JOIN table second (since the source isn't always necessarily with a FROM clause):
```sql
  -- Good
  FROM source
  LEFT JOIN other_source
    ON source.id = other_source.id
  LEFT JOIN different_source
    ON other_source.id = different_source.id
  WHERE ...

  -- Bad
  FROM source
  LEFT JOIN other_source
    ON other_source.id = source.id
  LEFT JOIN different_source
    ON different_source.id = other_source.id
  WHERE ...
```

The motivation is that we already know the JOIN table from the JOIN clause so we want to be able to quickly scan for the SOURCE table.
</issue_description>

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me carefully read and understand the issue description:

1. The goal is to implement a NEW RULE in SQLFluff that enforces a specific ordering convention in JOIN ON clauses
2. The rule should enforce that in ON clauses:
   - The source table (the table from the FROM clause or previous JOIN) should appear FIRST
   - The join table (the table being joined) should appear SECOND
3. Examples provided:
   - GOOD: FROM source LEFT JOIN other_source ON source.id = other_source.id
   - BAD: FROM source LEFT JOIN other_source ON other_source.id = source.id
4. This applies to all JOIN types (LEFT JOIN, INNER JOIN, etc.)

Key technical details:
- This is about JOIN ON clause ordering convention
- The source table should come before the join table in equality comparisons
- The motivation is readability - since we already know the JOIN table from the JOIN clause, we want to quickly scan for the SOURCE table

Steps to reproduce would be:
1. Have SQL with JOIN clauses where the join table appears first in the ON condition
2. Run SQLFluff linting
3. Expect it to flag violations of this ordering convention

Best practices for implementation:
- This needs to be a new linting rule
- Should integrate with SQLFluff's existing rule system
- Need to properly parse JOIN statements and ON conditions
- Should handle various JOIN types
- Should be configurable (as most SQLFluff rules are)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.1-1.3 Problem Analysis

The issue describes a **new linting rule** requirement for SQLFluff that enforces a specific ordering convention in JOIN ON clauses:

**Best practices/conventions from the examples:**
- In ON clauses, the **source table** (FROM table or previous JOIN table) should appear **first** 
- The **join table** (currently joined table) should appear **second**
- This improves readability by allowing quick scanning for source tables

**Technical details:**
- Rule applies to all JOIN types (LEFT JOIN, INNER JOIN, etc.)
- Focuses on equality comparisons in ON clauses
- Source identification: FROM table for first JOIN, previous JOIN table for subsequent JOINs

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Create SQL with JOIN clauses violating the ordering convention
2. Run SQLFluff linting  
3. Expect violations to be flagged

### 1.5 Best Practices for Implementation
- Create new linting rule following SQLFluff conventions
- Integrate with existing rule system and configuration
- Handle various JOIN scenarios correctly
- Provide clear violation messages

    18	**SQLFluff** is a dialect-flexible and configurable SQL linter. Designed
    19	with ELT applications in mind, **SQLFluff** also works with Jinja templating
    20	and dbt. **SQLFluff** will auto-fix most linting errors, allowing you to focus
    21	your time on what matters.
    22	
    23	## Dialects Supported
    24	
    25	Although SQL is reasonably consistent in its implementations, there are several
    26	different dialects available with variations of syntax and grammar. **SQLFluff**
    27	currently supports the following SQL dialects (though perhaps not in full):
    28	
    29	- ANSI SQL - this is the base version and on occasion may not strictly follow
    30	  the ANSI/ISO SQL definition

    34	- [Databricks](https://databricks.com/) (note: this extends the `sparksql` dialect with
    35	  [Unity Catalog](https://docs.databricks.com/data-governance/unity-catalog/index.html) syntax).
    36	- [Db2](https://www.ibm.com/analytics/db2)
    37	- [DuckDB](https://duckdb.org/)
    38	- [Exasol](https://www.exasol.com/)

     1	"""Implementation of Rule ST07."""
     2	from typing import List, Optional, Tuple
     3	from sqlfluff.core.parser.segments.base import BaseSegment
     4	from sqlfluff.core.parser.segments.raw import (
     5	    KeywordSegment,
     6	    SymbolSegment,
     7	    WhitespaceSegment,
     8	)
     9	from sqlfluff.core.rules import BaseRule, LintFix, LintResult, RuleContext
    10	from sqlfluff.core.rules.crawlers import SegmentSeekerCrawler
    11	from sqlfluff.utils.functional import Segments, sp, FunctionalContext
    12	from sqlfluff.utils.analysis.select import get_select_statement_info
    13	from sqlfluff.dialects.dialect_ansi import ColumnReferenceSegment, IdentifierSegment
    14	
    15	
    16	class Rule_ST07(BaseRule):
    17	    """Prefer specifying join keys instead of using ``USING``.
    18	
    19	    .. note::
    20	       This rule was originally taken from the `dbt Style Guide
    21	       <https://github.com/dbt-labs/corp/blob/ main/dbt_style_guide.md>`_
    22	       which notes that:
    23	
    24	        Certain warehouses have inconsistencies in ``USING``
    25	        results (specifically Snowflake).
    26	
    27	       In fact `dbt removed it from their style guide in February 2022
    28	       <https://github.com/dbt-labs/corp/pull/58>`_. However, some like the
    29	       rule, so for now we will keep it in SQLFluff, but encourage those that
    30	       do not find value in the rule, to turn it off.
    31	
    32	    .. note::
    33	
    34	       This rule is disabled for ClickHouse as it supports ``USING`` without
    35	       brackets which this rule does not support.
    36	
    37	    **Anti-pattern**
    38	
    39	    .. code-block:: sql
    40	
    41	        SELECT
    42	            table_a.field_1,
    43	            table_b.field_2
    44	        FROM
    45	            table_a
    46	        INNER JOIN table_b USING (id)
    47	
    48	    **Best practice**
    49	
    50	    Specify the keys directly
    51	
    52	    .. code-block:: sql
    53	
    54	        SELECT
    55	            table_a.field_1,
    56	            table_b.field_2
    57	        FROM
    58	            table_a
    59	        INNER JOIN table_b
    60	            ON table_a.id = table_b.id
    61	
    62	    """
    63	
    64	    name = "structure.using"
    65	    aliases = ("L032",)
    66	    groups: Tuple[str, ...] = ("all", "structure")
    67	    crawl_behaviour = SegmentSeekerCrawler({"join_clause"})
    68	    is_fix_compatible = True
    69	    _dialects_disabled_by_default = [
    70	        "clickhouse",
    71	    ]
    72	
    73	    def _eval(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[LintResult]:
    74	        if context.dialect.name in self._dialects_disabled_by_default:
    75	            return LintResult()
    76	
    77	        """Look for USING in a join clause."""
    78	        segment = FunctionalContext(context).segment
    79	        parent_stack = FunctionalContext(context).parent_stack
    80	        # We are not concerned with non join clauses
    81	        assert context.segment.is_type("join_clause")
    82	
    83	        using_anchor = segment.children(sp.is_keyword("using")).first()
    84	        # If there is no evidence of a USING then we exit
    85	        if len(using_anchor) == 0:
    86	            return None
    87	
    88	        anchor = using_anchor.get()
    89	        description = "Found USING statement. Expected only ON statements."
    90	        # All returns from here out will be some form of linting error.
    91	        # we prepare the variable here
    92	        unfixable_result = LintResult(
    93	            anchor=anchor,
    94	            description=description,
    95	        )
    96	
    97	        tables_in_join = parent_stack.last().children(
    98	            sp.is_type("join_clause", "from_expression_element")
    99	        )
   100	
   101	        # We can only safely fix the first join clause
   102	        if segment.get(0) != tables_in_join.get(1):
   103	            return unfixable_result
   104	
   105	        parent_select = parent_stack.last(sp.is_type("select_statement")).get()
   106	        if not parent_select:  # pragma: no cover
   107	            return unfixable_result
   108	
   109	        select_info = get_select_statement_info(parent_select, context.dialect)
   110	        table_aliases = [
   111	            ta
   112	            for ta in (select_info.table_aliases if select_info else [])
   113	            if ta.ref_str
   114	        ]
   115	        if len(table_aliases) < 2:
   116	            return unfixable_result
   117	
   118	        to_delete, insert_after_anchor = _extract_deletion_sequence_and_anchor(segment)
   119	
   120	        table_a, table_b = table_aliases[:2]
   121	        edit_segments = [
   122	            KeywordSegment(raw="ON"),
   123	            WhitespaceSegment(raw=" "),
   124	        ] + _generate_join_conditions(
   125	            table_a.ref_str,
   126	            table_b.ref_str,
   127	            _extract_cols_from_using(segment, using_anchor),
   128	        )
   129	
   130	        assert table_a.segment
   131	        assert table_b.segment
   132	        fixes = [
   133	            LintFix.create_before(
   134	                anchor_segment=insert_after_anchor,
   135	                source=[table_a.segment, table_b.segment],
   136	                edit_segments=edit_segments,
   137	            ),
   138	            *[LintFix.delete(seg) for seg in to_delete],
   139	        ]
   140	        return LintResult(
   141	            anchor=anchor,
   142	            description=description,
   143	            fixes=fixes,
   144	        )
   145	
   146	
   147	def _extract_cols_from_using(join_clause: Segments, using_segs: Segments) -> List[str]:
   148	    # First bracket after the USING keyword, then find ids
   149	    using_cols: List[str] = (
   150	        join_clause.children()
   151	        .select(start_seg=using_segs[0], select_if=sp.is_type("bracketed"))
   152	        .first()
   153	        .children(sp.is_type("identifier"))
   154	        .apply(lambda el: el.raw)
   155	    )
   156	    return using_cols
   157	
   158	
   159	def _generate_join_conditions(
   160	    table_a_ref: str, table_b_ref: str, columns: List[str]
   161	) -> List[BaseSegment]:
   162	    edit_segments: List[BaseSegment] = []
   163	    for col in columns:
   164	        edit_segments = edit_segments + [
   165	            _create_col_reference(
   166	                table_a_ref,
   167	                col,
   168	            ),
   169	            WhitespaceSegment(raw=" "),
   170	            SymbolSegment(raw="="),
   171	            WhitespaceSegment(raw=" "),
   172	            _create_col_reference(
   173	                table_b_ref,
   174	                col,
   175	            ),
   176	            WhitespaceSegment(raw=" "),
   177	            KeywordSegment(raw="AND"),
   178	            WhitespaceSegment(raw=" "),
   179	        ]
   180	
   181	    # Trim the " " "AND" " " at the end
   182	    return edit_segments[:-3]
   183	
   184	
   185	SequenceAndAnchorRes = Tuple[List[BaseSegment], BaseSegment]
   186	
   187	
   188	def _extract_deletion_sequence_and_anchor(
   189	    join_clause: Segments,
   190	) -> SequenceAndAnchorRes:
   191	    insert_anchor: Optional[BaseSegment] = None
   192	    to_delete: List[BaseSegment] = []
   193	    for seg in join_clause.children():
   194	        if seg.raw_upper == "USING":
   195	            # Start collecting once we hit USING
   196	            to_delete.append(seg)
   197	            continue
   198	
   199	        if len(to_delete) == 0:
   200	            # Skip if we haven't started collecting
   201	            continue
   202	
   203	        if to_delete[-1].is_type("bracketed"):
   204	            # terminate when we hit the brackets
   205	            insert_anchor = seg
   206	            break
   207	
   208	        to_delete.append(seg)
   209	
   210	    assert insert_anchor, "Insert Anchor must be present at this point"
   211	    return to_delete, insert_anchor
   212	
   213	
   214	def _create_col_reference(table_ref: str, column_name: str) -> ColumnReferenceSegment:
   215	    segments = (
   216	        IdentifierSegment(raw=table_ref, type="naked_identifier"),
   217	        SymbolSegment(raw=".", type="symbol"),
   218	        IdentifierSegment(raw=column_name, type="naked_identifier"),
   219	    )
   220	    return ColumnReferenceSegment(segments=segments, pos_marker=None)
   221

     8	class Rule_CV08(BaseRule):
     9	    """Use ``LEFT JOIN`` instead of ``RIGHT JOIN``.
    10	
    11	    **Anti-pattern**
    12	
    13	    ``RIGHT JOIN`` is used.
    14	
    15	    .. code-block:: sql
    16	       :force:
    17	
    18	        SELECT
    19	            foo.col1,
    20	            bar.col2
    21	        FROM foo
    22	        RIGHT JOIN bar
    23	            ON foo.bar_id = bar.id;
    24	
    25	    **Best practice**
    26	
    27	    Refactor and use ``LEFT JOIN`` instead.
    28	
    29	    .. code-block:: sql
    30	       :force:
    31	
    32	        SELECT
    33	            foo.col1,
    34	            bar.col2
    35	        FROM bar
    36	        LEFT JOIN foo
    37	            ON foo.bar_id = bar.id;
    38	    """
    39	
    40	    name = "convention.left_join"
    41	    aliases = ("L055",)
    42	    groups = ("all", "convention")
    43	    crawl_behaviour = SegmentSeekerCrawler({"join_clause"})
    44	
    45	    def _eval(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[LintResult]:
    46	        """Use LEFT JOIN instead of RIGHT JOIN."""
    47	        # We are only interested in JOIN clauses.
    48	        assert context.segment.is_type("join_clause")
    49	
    50	        # Identify if RIGHT JOIN is present.
    51	        if {"RIGHT", "JOIN"}.issubset(
    52	            {segment.raw_upper for segment in context.segment.segments}
    53	        ):
    54	            return LintResult(context.segment.segments[0])
    55	
    56	        return None

     1	"""Runs the rule test cases."""
     2	import os
     3	import logging
     4	import pytest
     5	from sqlfluff.utils.testing.rules import (
     6	    load_test_cases,
     7	    rules__test_helper,
     8	    get_rule_from_set,
     9	)
    10	from sqlfluff.core.config import FluffConfig
    11	
    12	
    13	def pytest_generate_tests(metafunc):
    14	    """Generate tests, optionally by rule_id."""
    15	    rule_id = metafunc.config.getoption("rule_id")
    16	    ids, test_cases = load_test_cases(
    17	        test_cases_path=os.path.join(
    18	            "test/fixtures/rules/std_rule_cases", f"{rule_id}.yml"
    19	        )
    20	    )
    21	    if "test_case" in metafunc.fixturenames:
    22	        metafunc.parametrize("test_case", test_cases, ids=ids)
    23	
    24	
    25	@pytest.mark.integration
    26	@pytest.mark.rules_suite
    27	def test__rule_test_case(test_case, caplog):
    28	    """Run the tests."""
    29	    with caplog.at_level(logging.DEBUG, logger="sqlfluff.rules"):
    30	        with caplog.at_level(logging.DEBUG, logger="sqlfluff.linter"):
    31	            res = rules__test_helper(test_case)
    32	            if res is not None and res != test_case.fail_str:
    33	                cfg = FluffConfig(configs=test_case.configs)
    34	                rule = get_rule_from_set(test_case.rule, config=cfg)
    35	                assert rule.is_fix_compatible, f"Rule {test_case.rule} returned "
    36	                'fixes but does not specify "is_fix_compatible = True".'
    37	
    38	
    39	def test__rule_test_global_config():
    40	    """Test global config in rule test cases."""
    41	    ids, test_cases = load_test_cases(
    42	        os.path.join("test/fixtures/rules/R001_global_config_test.yml")
    43	    )
    44	    assert len(test_cases) == 2
    45	    # tc1: overwrites global config
    46	    assert test_cases[0].configs["core"]["dialect"] == "ansi"
    47	    # tc2: global config is used
    48	    assert test_cases[1].configs["core"]["dialect"] == "exasol"
    49

## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Creating a Reproduction Script

Let me first create a simple script to test the current behavior and understand how SQLFluff processes JOIN clauses:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/test_join_order.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Test script to reproduce the JOIN ON clause ordering issue."""

import sqlfluff

def test_current_behavior():
    """Test current SQLFluff behavior with JOIN ON clause ordering."""
    
    # Good case - source table first
    good_sql = """
    SELECT *
    FROM source
    LEFT JOIN other_source
      ON source.id = other_source.id
    WHERE condition = 1;
    """
    
    # Bad case - join table first  
    bad_sql = """
    SELECT *
    FROM source
    LEFT JOIN other_source
      ON other_source.id = source.id
    WHERE condition = 1;
    """
    
    # Complex case with multiple joins
    complex_good_sql = """
    SELECT *
    FROM source
    LEFT JOIN other_source
      ON source.id = other_source.id
    LEFT JOIN different_source
      ON other_source.id = different_source.id
    WHERE condition = 1;
    """
    
    # Complex bad case
    complex_bad_sql = """
    SELECT *
    FROM source
    LEFT JOIN other_source
      ON other_source.id = source.id
    LEFT JOIN different_source
      ON different_source.id = other_source.id
    WHERE condition = 1;
    """
    
    print("=== Testing Current Behavior ===")
    print("\nGood SQL (source first):")
    print(good_sql.strip())
    result = sqlfluff.lint(good_sql, dialect='ansi')
    print(f"Violations: {len(result)}")
    for v in result:
        print(f"  - {v['code']}: {v['description']}")
    
    print("\nBad SQL (join table first):")
    print(bad_sql.strip())
    result = sqlfluff.lint(bad_sql, dialect='ansi')
    print(f"Violations: {len(result)}")
    for v in result:
        print(f"  - {v['code']}: {v['description']}")
        
    print("\nComplex Good SQL:")
    print(complex_good_sql.strip())
    result = sqlfluff.lint(complex_good_sql, dialect='ansi')
    print(f"Violations: {len(result)}")
    for v in result:
        print(f"  - {v['code']}: {v['description']}")
        
    print("\nComplex Bad SQL:")
    print(complex_bad_sql.strip())
    result = sqlfluff.lint(complex_bad_sql, dialect='ansi')
    print(f"Violations: {len(result)}")
    for v in result:
        print(f"  - {v['code']}: {v['description']}")

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Problem Statement and Solution Plan<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me clearly state the problem and solution approach:

**Problem:** 
There is no SQLFluff rule that enforces the convention that in JOIN ON clauses, the source table (FROM table or previous JOIN table) should appear first, followed by the join table (currently joined table).

**Where the problem is located:**
This is a missing feature - there is no rule implemented for this specific convention.

**How the test reproduces the issue:**
My test script shows that both valid and invalid JOIN ON clause orderings pass without violations, proving the rule doesn't exist.

**Best practices for the fix:**
1. Create a new rule following SQLFluff conventions (CV12 in the convention category)
2. The rule should crawl join_clause segments 
3. For each join_clause, examine the join_on_condition child segment
4. Parse the ON condition expression to identify table.column references
5. Determine which table is the source (FROM table or previous JOIN) vs join table (current JOIN)
6. Flag violations where the join table appears before the source table in equality comparisons
7. Provide clear violation messages
8. Follow existing rule patterns for configuration and testing

**How to fix the problem:**
1. Create a new rule file: src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py
2. Implement logic to:
   - Identify join clauses
   - Parse ON conditions 
   - Extract table references from equality expressions
   - Determine source vs join table for each JOIN
   - Check ordering convention
3. Create test cases in test/fixtures/rules/std_rule_cases/CV12.yml
4. Register the rule appropriately</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement
There is no SQLFluff rule that enforces the GitLab SQL style guide convention requiring JOIN ON clauses to specify the source table first and join table second.

### 5.2 Problem Location
This is a missing feature - no rule exists in `/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/src/sqlfluff/rules/` to detect this JOIN ON clause ordering convention.

### 5.3 Test Reproduction
My test scripts confirm that both compliant and non-compliant JOIN ON clause orderings currently pass without violations, proving the rule doesn't exist.

### 5.4 Best Practices
- Create new rule following SQLFluff conventions (CV12 in convention category)
- Crawl `join_clause` segments 
- Parse `join_on_condition` to analyze table reference ordering
- Provide clear violation messages
- Follow existing rule patterns for configuration and testing

### 5.5 Solution Approach
1. Create `src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py` implementing the new rule
2. Add test cases in `test/fixtures/rules/std_rule_cases/CV12.yml`
3. Implement logic to identify source vs join tables and check ordering convention

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION: Creating the New Rule

Let me first examine how to identify source tables vs join tables. I need to understand the structure better:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/debug_join_structure.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Debug script to understand JOIN structure in SQLFluff."""

import sqlfluff

def debug_join_parsing():
    """Debug how SQLFluff parses JOIN structures."""
    
    sql = "SELECT * FROM source LEFT JOIN other_source ON source.id = other_source.id;"
    
    # Parse the SQL
    parsed = sqlfluff.parse(sql, dialect='ansi')
    
    print("=== Parsed Structure ===")
    print(parsed.tree.stringify())
    
    # Find join clauses
    print("\n=== Join Clauses ===")
    for join_clause in parsed.tree.recursive_crawl("join_clause"):
        print(f"Join Clause: {join_clause}")
        print(f"  Type: {join_clause.get_type()}")
        print(f"  Raw: {join_clause.raw}")
        
        # Look at children
        print("  Children:")
        for child in join_clause.segments:
            print(f"    - {child.get_type()}: {child.raw}")
            
        # Look for ON condition
        print("  ON Conditions:")
        for on_condition in join_clause.recursive_crawl("join_on_condition"):
            print(f"    ON Condition: {on_condition}")
            print(f"      Raw: {on_condition.raw}")
            print(f"      Children:")
            for child in on_condition.segments:
                print(f"        - {child.get_type()}: {child.raw}")
                
            # Look at the expression inside ON
            for expr in on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"):
                print(f"      Expression: {expr}")
                print(f"        Raw: {expr.raw}")
                print(f"        Children:")
                for child in expr.segments:
                    print(f"          - {child.get_type()}: {child.raw}")
                    
                # Look for column references
                for col_ref in expr.recursive_crawl("column_reference"):
                    print(f"        Column Reference: {col_ref}")
                    print(f"          Raw: {col_ref.raw}")

if __name__ == "__main__":

"""Debug script to understand JOIN structure in SQLFluff."""

import sqlfluff
from sqlfluff.api import parse

def debug_join_parsing():
    """Debug how SQLFluff parses JOIN structures."""
    
    sql = "SELECT * FROM source LEFT JOIN other_source ON source.id = other_source.id;"
    
    # Parse the SQL
    parsed = parse(sql, dialect='ansi')
    
    print("=== Parsed Structure ===")

Based on the pattern, I'll create CV12.py:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>"""Implementation of Rule CV12."""
from typing import Optional, List, Set
from sqlfluff.core.rules import BaseRule, LintResult, RuleContext
from sqlfluff.core.rules.crawlers import SegmentSeekerCrawler


class Rule_CV12(BaseRule):
    """In ``ON`` clause of ``JOIN`` statement, specify source table first and join table second.

    **Anti-pattern**

    In the ``ON`` clause, the join table is specified before the source table.

    .. code-block:: sql
       :force:

        SELECT
            source.col1,
            other_source.col2
        FROM source
        LEFT JOIN other_source
            ON other_source.id = source.id;

    **Best practice**

    In the ``ON`` clause, specify the source table before the join table.

    .. code-block:: sql
       :force:

        SELECT
            source.col1,
            other_source.col2
        FROM source
        LEFT JOIN other_source
            ON source.id = other_source.id;
    """

    name = "convention.join_on_clause_table_order"
    aliases = ()
    groups = ("all", "convention")
    crawl_behaviour = SegmentSeekerCrawler({"join_clause"})
    is_fix_compatible = False

    def _eval(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[LintResult]:
        """Check JOIN ON clause table ordering."""
        # We are only interested in JOIN clauses.
        assert context.segment.is_type("join_clause")

        # Find the join table name (the table being joined)
        join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(context.segment)
        if not join_table_name:
            return None

        # Find the ON condition
        on_conditions = list(context.segment.recursive_crawl("join_on_condition"))
        if not on_conditions:
            return None

        on_condition = on_conditions[0]
        
        # Get the parent FROM clause to determine the source table
        source_table_name = self._get_source_table_name(context)
        if not source_table_name:
            return None

        # Check the ordering in equality expressions within the ON condition
        violations = self._check_equality_ordering(on_condition, source_table_name, join_table_name)
        
        if violations:
            # Return the first violation
            return LintResult(
                anchor=violations[0],
                description=f"In join ON clause, source table '{source_table_name}' should appear before join table '{join_table_name}'."
            )

        return None

    def _get_join_table_name(self, join_clause) -> Optional[str]:
        """Extract the name of the table being joined."""
        # Look for the table reference in the join clause
        from_expression_elements = list(join_clause.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))
        if not from_expression_elements:
            return None
            
        # Get the table expression
        table_expressions = list(from_expression_elements[0].recursive_crawl("table_expression"))
        if not table_expressions:
            return None
            
        # Get the table reference
        table_references = list(table_expressions[0].recursive_crawl("table_reference"))
        if not table_references:
            return None
            
        # Get the naked identifier (table name)
        identifiers = list(table_references[0].recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
        if not identifiers:
            return None
            
        return identifiers[0].raw.lower()

    def _get_source_table_name(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[str]:
        """Get the source table name for this join (previous table in FROM clause)."""
        # Walk up the parent stack to find the FROM clause
        parent_stack = list(context.parent_stack)
        
        # Find the containing select statement
        select_statement = None
        for parent in reversed(parent_stack):
            if parent.get_type() == "select_statement":
                select_statement = parent
                break
                
        if not select_statement:
            return None
            
        # Get all from expression elements to understand table order
        from_expressions = list(select_statement.recursive_crawl("from_expression"))
        if not from_expressions:
            return None
            
        # Get all table references in order
        table_names = []
        for from_expr in from_expressions:
            # Get direct table references (FROM table)
            from_elements = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))
            for element in from_elements:
                table_refs = list(element.recursive_crawl("table_reference"))
                for table_ref in table_refs:
                    identifiers = list(table_ref.recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
                    if identifiers:
                        table_names.append(identifiers[0].raw.lower())
            
            # Get join table references  
            join_clauses = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("join_clause"))
            for join_clause in join_clauses:
                if join_clause is context.segment:
                    # This is our current join clause, so the last table name we found
                    # is the source table
                    if table_names:
                        return table_names[-1]
                else:
                    # Previous join clause, add its table to our list
                    join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(join_clause)
                    if join_table_name:
                        table_names.append(join_table_name)
                        
        return None

    def _check_equality_ordering(self, on_condition, source_table: str, join_table: str) -> List:
        """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
        violations = []
        
        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
        for expr in expressions:
            # Look for comparison operators (=)
            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
            if not comparison_ops:
                continue
                
            # Get the left and right sides of the comparison
            children = expr.segments
            equals_op_index = None
            for i, child in enumerate(children):
                if child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
                    equals_op_index = i
                    break
                    
            if equals_op_index is None:
                continue
                
            # Get left and right operands
            if equals_op_index > 0 and equals_op_index < len(children) - 1:
                left_operand = children[equals_op_index - 1]
                right_operand = children[equals_op_index + 1]
                
                # Check if both operands are column references
                left_columns = list(left_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                right_columns = list(right_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                
                if left_columns and right_columns:
                    # Extract table names from column references
                    left_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(left_columns[0])
                    right_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(right_columns[0])
                    
                    # Check if this is a join between source and join table
                    if (left_table and right_table and 
                        ((left_table == source_table and right_table == join_table) or
                         (left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table))):
                        
                        # Violation if join table is on the left and source table is on the right
                        if left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table:
                            violations.append(expr)
                            
        return violations

    def _extract_table_name_from_column(self, column_ref) -> Optional[str]:
        """Extract table name from a column reference."""
        identifiers = list(column_ref.recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
        if len(identifiers) >= 2:
            # Format: table.column
            return identifiers[0].raw.lower()
        elif len(identifiers) == 1:
            # Could be just column name without table prefix
            # In this case we can't determine the table definitively
            pass
        return None</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now let me test this rule with my simple test:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/test_new_rule.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

from typing import Optional, List, Set
from sqlfluff.core.rules import BaseRule, LintResult, RuleContext
from sqlfluff.core.rules.crawlers import SegmentSeekerCrawler


class Rule_CV12(BaseRule):
    """In ``ON`` clause of ``JOIN`` statement, specify source table first and join table second.

    **Anti-pattern**

    In the ``ON`` clause, the join table is specified before the source table.

    .. code-block:: sql
       :force:

        SELECT
            source.col1,
            other_source.col2
        FROM source
        LEFT JOIN other_source
            ON other_source.id = source.id;

    **Best practice**

    In the ``ON`` clause, specify the source table before the join table.

    .. code-block:: sql
       :force:

        SELECT
            source.col1,
            other_source.col2
        FROM source
        LEFT JOIN other_source
            ON source.id = other_source.id;
    """

    name = "convention.join_on_clause_table_order"
    aliases = ()
    groups = ("all", "convention")
    crawl_behaviour = SegmentSeekerCrawler({"join_clause"})
    is_fix_compatible = False

    def _eval(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[LintResult]:
        """Check JOIN ON clause table ordering."""
        # We are only interested in JOIN clauses.
        assert context.segment.is_type("join_clause")

        # Debug print
        # print(f"Processing join clause: {context.segment.raw}")

        # Find the join table name (the table being joined)
        join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(context.segment)
        if not join_table_name:
            # print("Could not determine join table name")
            return None

        # Find the ON condition
        on_conditions = list(context.segment.recursive_crawl("join_on_condition"))
        if not on_conditions:
            # print("No ON condition found")
            return None

        on_condition = on_conditions[0]
        
        # Get the parent FROM clause to determine the source table
        source_table_name = self._get_source_table_name(context)
        if not source_table_name:
            # print("Could not determine source table name")
            return None

        # Debug info
        # print(f"Source table: {source_table_name}, Join table: {join_table_name}")

        # Check the ordering in equality expressions within the ON condition
        violations = self._check_equality_ordering(on_condition, source_table_name, join_table_name)
        
        if violations:
            # Return the first violation
            return LintResult(
                anchor=violations[0],
                description=f"In join ON clause, source table '{source_table_name}' should appear before join table '{join_table_name}'."
            )

        return None

    def _get_join_table_name(self, join_clause) -> Optional[str]:
        """Extract the name of the table being joined."""
        # Look for the table reference in the join clause
        from_expression_elements = list(join_clause.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))
        if not from_expression_elements:
            return None
            
        # Get the table expression
        table_expressions = list(from_expression_elements[0].recursive_crawl("table_expression"))
        if not table_expressions:
            return None
            
        # Get the table reference
        table_references = list(table_expressions[0].recursive_crawl("table_reference"))
        if not table_references:
            return None
            
        # Get the naked identifier (table name)
        identifiers = list(table_references[0].recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
        if not identifiers:
            return None
            
        return identifiers[0].raw.lower()

    def _get_source_table_name(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[str]:
        """Get the source table name for this join (previous table in FROM clause)."""
        # Walk up the parent stack to find the FROM clause
        parent_stack = list(context.parent_stack)
        
        # Find the containing select statement
        select_statement = None
        for parent in reversed(parent_stack):
            if parent.get_type() == "select_statement":
                select_statement = parent
                break
                
        if not select_statement:
            return None
            
        # Get all from expression elements to understand table order
        from_expressions = list(select_statement.recursive_crawl("from_expression"))
        if not from_expressions:
            return None
            
        # Get all table references in order
        table_names = []
        for from_expr in from_expressions:
            # Get direct table references (FROM table)
            from_elements = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))
            for element in from_elements:
                table_refs = list(element.recursive_crawl("table_reference"))
                for table_ref in table_refs:
                    identifiers = list(table_ref.recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
                    if identifiers:
                        table_names.append(identifiers[0].raw.lower())
            
            # Get join table references  
            join_clauses = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("join_clause"))
            for join_clause in join_clauses:
                if join_clause is context.segment:
                    # This is our current join clause, so the last table name we found
                    # is the source table
                    if table_names:
                        return table_names[-1]
                else:
                    # Previous join clause, add its table to our list
                    join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(join_clause)
                    if join_table_name:
                        table_names.append(join_table_name)
                        
        return None

    def _check_equality_ordering(self, on_condition, source_table: str, join_table: str) -> List:
        """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
        violations = []
        
        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
        for expr in expressions:
            # Look for comparison operators (=)
            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
            if not comparison_ops:
                continue
                
            # Get the left and right sides of the comparison
            children = expr.segments
            equals_op_index = None
            for i, child in enumerate(children):
                if hasattr(child, 'get_type') and child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
                    equals_op_index = i
                    break
                    
            if equals_op_index is None:
                continue
                
            # Get left and right operands
            if equals_op_index > 0 and equals_op_index < len(children) - 1:
                left_operand = children[equals_op_index - 1]
                right_operand = children[equals_op_index + 1]
                
                # Check if both operands are column references
                left_columns = list(left_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                right_columns = list(right_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                
                if left_columns and right_columns:
                    # Extract table names from column references
                    left_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(left_columns[0])
                    right_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(right_columns[0])
                    
                    # Debug info
                    # print(f"Comparing: {left_table} = {right_table}")
                    
                    # Check if this is a join between source and join table
                    if (left_table and right_table and 
                        ((left_table == source_table and right_table == join_table) or
                         (left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table))):
                        
                        # Violation if join table is on the left and source table is on the right
                        if left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table:
                            violations.append(expr)
                            
        return violations

    def _extract_table_name_from_column(self, column_ref) -> Optional[str]:
        """Extract table name from a column reference."""
        identifiers = list(column_ref.recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
        if len(identifiers) >= 2:
            # Format: table.column
            return identifiers[0].raw.lower()
        elif len(identifiers) == 1:
            # Could be just column name without table prefix
            # In this case we can't determine the table definitively
            pass
        return None</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>"""Implementation of Rule CV12."""

from sqlfluff.core.rules import BaseRule, LintResult, RuleContext

class Rule_CV12(BaseRule):
    """In ``ON`` clause of ``JOIN`` statement, specify source table first and join table second.

    **Anti-pattern**

    .. code-block:: sql

    aliases = ()

    crawl_behaviour = SegmentSeekerCrawler({"join_clause"})

    def _eval(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[LintResult]:
        """Check JOIN ON clause table ordering."""
        # We are only interested in JOIN clauses.
        assert context.segment.is_type("join_clause")

        if not on_conditions:

                description=f"In join ON clause, source table '{source_table_name}' should appear before join table '{join_table_name}'."

    def _get_source_table_name(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[str]:
        """Get the source table name for this join (previous table in FROM clause)."""
        # Walk up the parent stack to find the FROM clause
        parent_stack = list(context.parent_stack)

            from_elements = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))

    88	    def _get_join_table_name(self, join_clause) -> Optional[str]:
    89	        """Extract the name of the table being joined."""
    90	        # Look for the table reference in the join clause
    91	        from_expression_elements = list(join_clause.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))
    92	        if not from_expression_elements:
    93	            return None
    94	            
    95	        # Get the table expression
    96	        table_expressions = list(from_expression_elements[0].recursive_crawl("table_expression"))
    97	        if not table_expressions:
    98	            return None
    99	            
   100	        # Get the table reference
   101	        table_references = list(table_expressions[0].recursive_crawl("table_reference"))
   102	        if not table_references:
   103	            return None
   104	            
   105	        # Get the naked identifier (table name)
   106	        identifiers = list(table_references[0].recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
   107	        if not identifiers:
   108	            return None
   109	            
   110	        return identifiers[0].raw.lower()
   111	
   112	    def _get_source_table_name(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[str]:
   113	        """Get the source table name for this join (previous table in FROM clause)."""
   114	        # Walk up the parent stack to find the FROM clause
   115	        parent_stack = list(context.parent_stack)
   116	        
   117	        # Find the containing select statement
   118	        select_statement = None
   119	        for parent in reversed(parent_stack):
   120	            if parent.get_type() == "select_statement":
   121	                select_statement = parent
   122	                break
   123	                
   124	        if not select_statement:
   125	            return None
   126	            
   127	        # Get all from expression elements to understand table order
   128	        from_expressions = list(select_statement.recursive_crawl("from_expression"))
   129	        if not from_expressions:
   130	            return None
   131	            
   132	        # Get all table references in order
   133	        table_names = []
   134	        for from_expr in from_expressions:
   135	            # Get direct table references (FROM table)
   136	            from_elements = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))
   137	            for element in from_elements:
   138	                table_refs = list(element.recursive_crawl("table_reference"))
   139	                for table_ref in table_refs:
   140	                    identifiers = list(table_ref.recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
   141	                    if identifiers:
   142	                        table_names.append(identifiers[0].raw.lower())
   143	            
   144	            # Get join table references  
   145	            join_clauses = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("join_clause"))
   146	            for join_clause in join_clauses:
   147	                if join_clause is context.segment:
   148	                    # This is our current join clause, so the last table name we found
   149	                    # is the source table
   150	                    if table_names:
   151	                        return table_names[-1]
   152	                else:
   153	                    # Previous join clause, add its table to our list
   154	                    join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(join_clause)
   155	                    if join_table_name:
   156	                        table_names.append(join_table_name)
   157	                        
   158	        return None
   159	
   160	    def _check_equality_ordering(self, on_condition, source_table: str, join_table: str) -> List:
   161	        """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
   162	        violations = []
   163	        
   164	        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
   165	        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
   166	        for expr in expressions:
   167	            # Look for comparison operators (=)
   168	            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
   169	            if not comparison_ops:
   170	                continue
   171	                
   172	            # Get the left and right sides of the comparison
   173	            children = expr.segments
   174	            equals_op_index = None
   175	            for i, child in enumerate(children):
   176	                if hasattr(child, 'get_type') and child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
   177	                    equals_op_index = i
   178	                    break
   179	                    
   180	            if equals_op_index is None:
   181	                continue
   182	                
   183	            # Get left and right operands
   184	            if equals_op_index > 0 and equals_op_index < len(children) - 1:
   185	                left_operand = children[equals_op_index - 1]
   186	                right_operand = children[equals_op_index + 1]
   187	                
   188	                # Check if both operands are column references
   189	                left_columns = list(left_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
   190	                right_columns = list(right_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
   191	                
   192	                if left_columns and right_columns:
   193	                    # Extract table names from column references
   194	                    left_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(left_columns[0])
   195	                    right_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(right_columns[0])
   196	                    
   197	                    # Debug info
   198	                    # print(f"Comparing: {left_table} = {right_table}")
   199	                    
   200	                    # Check if this is a join between source and join table
   201	                    if (left_table and right_table and 
   202	                        ((left_table == source_table and right_table == join_table) or
   203	                         (left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table))):
   204	                        
   205	                        # Violation if join table is on the left and source table is on the right
   206	                        if left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table:
   207	                            violations.append(expr)
   208	                            
   209	        return violations
   210	
   211	    def _extract_table_name_from_column(self, column_ref) -> Optional[str]:
   212	        """Extract table name from a column reference."""
   213	        identifiers = list(column_ref.recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
   214	        if len(identifiers) >= 2:
   215	            # Format: table.column
   216	            return identifiers[0].raw.lower()
   217	        elif len(identifiers) == 1:
   218	            # Could be just column name without table prefix
   219	            # In this case we can't determine the table definitively
   220	            pass
   221	        return None

from typing import Optional, List, Set
from sqlfluff.core.rules import BaseRule, LintResult, RuleContext
from sqlfluff.core.rules.crawlers import SegmentSeekerCrawler


class Rule_CV12(BaseRule):
    """In ``ON`` clause of ``JOIN`` statement, specify source table first and join table second.

    **Anti-pattern**

    In the ``ON`` clause, the join table is specified before the source table.

    .. code-block:: sql
       :force:

        SELECT
            source.col1,
            other_source.col2
        FROM source
        LEFT JOIN other_source
            ON other_source.id = source.id;

    **Best practice**

    In the ``ON`` clause, specify the source table before the join table.

    .. code-block:: sql
       :force:

        SELECT
            source.col1,
            other_source.col2
        FROM source
        LEFT JOIN other_source
            ON source.id = other_source.id;
    """

    name = "convention.join_on_clause_table_order"
    aliases = ()
    groups = ("all", "convention")
    crawl_behaviour = SegmentSeekerCrawler({"join_clause"})
    is_fix_compatible = False

    def _eval(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[LintResult]:
        """Check JOIN ON clause table ordering."""
        # We are only interested in JOIN clauses.
        assert context.segment.is_type("join_clause")

        # Simple test - just return a violation for any JOIN to see if the rule is working
        # return LintResult(description="Test violation")
        
        # Find the join table name (the table being joined)
        join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(context.segment)
        if not join_table_name:
            return None

        # Find the ON condition
        on_conditions = list(context.segment.recursive_crawl("join_on_condition"))
        if not on_conditions:
            return None

        on_condition = on_conditions[0]
        
        # Get the parent FROM clause to determine the source table
        source_table_name = self._get_source_table_name(context)
        if not source_table_name:
            return None

        # Check the ordering in equality expressions within the ON condition
        violations = self._check_equality_ordering(on_condition, source_table_name, join_table_name)
        
        if violations:
            # Return the first violation
            return LintResult(
                anchor=violations[0],
                description=f"In join ON clause, source table '{source_table_name}' should appear before join table '{join_table_name}'."
            )

        return None

    def _get_join_table_name(self, join_clause) -> Optional[str]:
        """Extract the name of the table being joined."""
        # Look for the table reference in the join clause
        from_expression_elements = list(join_clause.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))
        if not from_expression_elements:
            return None
            
        # Get the table expression
        table_expressions = list(from_expression_elements[0].recursive_crawl("table_expression"))
        if not table_expressions:
            return None
            
        # Get the table reference
        table_references = list(table_expressions[0].recursive_crawl("table_reference"))
        if not table_references:
            return None
            
        # Get the naked identifier (table name)
        identifiers = list(table_references[0].recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
        if not identifiers:
            return None
            
        return identifiers[0].raw.lower()

    def _get_source_table_name(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[str]:
        """Get the source table name for this join (previous table in FROM clause)."""
        # Walk up the parent stack to find the FROM clause
        parent_stack = list(context.parent_stack)
        
        # Find the containing select statement
        select_statement = None
        for parent in reversed(parent_stack):
            if parent.get_type() == "select_statement":
                select_statement = parent
                break
                
        if not select_statement:
            return None
            
        # Get all from expression elements to understand table order
        from_expressions = list(select_statement.recursive_crawl("from_expression"))
        if not from_expressions:
            return None
            
        # Get all table references in order
        table_names = []
        for from_expr in from_expressions:
            # Get direct table references (FROM table)
            from_elements = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))
            for element in from_elements:
                table_refs = list(element.recursive_crawl("table_reference"))
                for table_ref in table_refs:
                    identifiers = list(table_ref.recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
                    if identifiers:
                        table_names.append(identifiers[0].raw.lower())
            
            # Get join table references  
            join_clauses = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("join_clause"))
            for join_clause in join_clauses:
                if join_clause is context.segment:
                    # This is our current join clause, so the last table name we found
                    # is the source table
                    if table_names:
                        return table_names[-1]
                else:
                    # Previous join clause, add its table to our list
                    join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(join_clause)
                    if join_table_name:
                        table_names.append(join_table_name)
                        
        return None

    def _check_equality_ordering(self, on_condition, source_table: str, join_table: str) -> List:
        """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
        violations = []
        
        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
        for expr in expressions:
            # Look for comparison operators (=)
            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
            if not comparison_ops:
                continue
                
            # Get the left and right sides of the comparison
            children = expr.segments if hasattr(expr, 'segments') else []
            equals_op_index = None
            for i, child in enumerate(children):
                if hasattr(child, 'get_type') and child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
                    equals_op_index = i
                    break
                    
            if equals_op_index is None:
                continue
                
            # Get left and right operands
            if equals_op_index > 0 and equals_op_index < len(children) - 1:
                left_operand = children[equals_op_index - 1]
                right_operand = children[equals_op_index + 1]
                
                # Check if both operands are column references
                left_columns = list(left_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                right_columns = list(right_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                
                if left_columns and right_columns:
                    # Extract table names from column references
                    left_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(left_columns[0])
                    right_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(right_columns[0])
                    
                    # Check if this is a join between source and join table
                    if (left_table and right_table and 
                        ((left_table == source_table and right_table == join_table) or
                         (left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table))):
                        
                        # Violation if join table is on the left and source table is on the right
                        if left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table:
                            violations.append(expr)
                            
        return violations

    def _extract_table_name_from_column(self, column_ref) -> Optional[str]:
        """Extract table name from a column reference."""
        identifiers = list(column_ref.recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
        if len(identifiers) >= 2:
            # Format: table.column
            return identifiers[0].raw.lower()
        elif len(identifiers) == 1:
            # Could be just column name without table prefix
            # In this case we can't determine the table definitively
            pass
        return None</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>"""Implementation of Rule CV12."""
from typing import Optional, List, Set
from sqlfluff.core.rules import BaseRule, LintResult, RuleContext
from sqlfluff.core.rules.crawlers import SegmentSeekerCrawler


class Rule_CV12(BaseRule):
    """In ``ON`` clause of ``JOIN`` statement, specify source table first and join table second.

    **Anti-pattern**

    In the ``ON`` clause, the join table is specified before the source table.

    .. code-block:: sql
       :force:

        SELECT
            source.col1,
            other_source.col2
        FROM source
        LEFT JOIN other_source
            ON other_source.id = source.id;

    **Best practice**

    In the ``ON`` clause, specify the source table before the join table.

    .. code-block:: sql
       :force:

        SELECT
            source.col1,
            other_source.col2
        FROM source
        LEFT JOIN other_source
            ON source.id = other_source.id;
    """

    name = "convention.join_on_clause_table_order"
    aliases = ()
    groups = ("all", "convention")
    crawl_behaviour = SegmentSeekerCrawler({"join_clause"})
    is_fix_compatible = False

    def _eval(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[LintResult]:
        """Check JOIN ON clause table ordering."""
        # We are only interested in JOIN clauses.
        assert context.segment.is_type("join_clause")

        # Debug print
        # print(f"Processing join clause: {context.segment.raw}")

        # Find the join table name (the table being joined)
        join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(context.segment)
        if not join_table_name:
            # print("Could not determine join table name")
            return None

        # Find the ON condition
        on_conditions = list(context.segment.recursive_crawl("join_on_condition"))
        if not on_conditions:
            # print("No ON condition found")
            return None

        on_condition = on_conditions[0]
        
        # Get the parent FROM clause to determine the source table
        source_table_name = self._get_source_table_name(context)
        if not source_table_name:
            # print("Could not determine source table name")
            return None

        # Debug info
        # print(f"Source table: {source_table_name}, Join table: {join_table_name}")

        # Check the ordering in equality expressions within the ON condition
        violations = self._check_equality_ordering(on_condition, source_table_name, join_table_name)
        
        if violations:
            # Return the first violation
            return LintResult(
                anchor=violations[0],
                description=f"In join ON clause, source table '{source_table_name}' should appear before join table '{join_table_name}'."
            )

        return None

    def _get_join_table_name(self, join_clause) -> Optional[str]:
        """Extract the name of the table being joined."""
        # Look for the table reference in the join clause
        from_expression_elements = list(join_clause.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))
        if not from_expression_elements:
            return None
            
        # Get the table expression
        table_expressions = list(from_expression_elements[0].recursive_crawl("table_expression"))
        if not table_expressions:
            return None
            
        # Get the table reference
        table_references = list(table_expressions[0].recursive_crawl("table_reference"))
        if not table_references:
            return None
            
        # Get the naked identifier (table name)
        identifiers = list(table_references[0].recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
        if not identifiers:
            return None
            
        return identifiers[0].raw.lower()

    def _get_source_table_name(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[str]:
        """Get the source table name for this join (previous table in FROM clause)."""
        # Walk up the parent stack to find the FROM clause
        parent_stack = list(context.parent_stack)
        
        # Find the containing select statement
        select_statement = None
        for parent in reversed(parent_stack):
            if parent.get_type() == "select_statement":
                select_statement = parent
                break
                
        if not select_statement:
            return None
            
        # Get all from expression elements to understand table order
        from_expressions = list(select_statement.recursive_crawl("from_expression"))
        if not from_expressions:
            return None
            
        # Get all table references in order
        table_names = []
        for from_expr in from_expressions:
            # Get direct table references (FROM table)
            from_elements = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))
            for element in from_elements:
                table_refs = list(element.recursive_crawl("table_reference"))
                for table_ref in table_refs:
                    identifiers = list(table_ref.recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
                    if identifiers:
                        table_names.append(identifiers[0].raw.lower())
            
            # Get join table references  
            join_clauses = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("join_clause"))
            for join_clause in join_clauses:
                if join_clause is context.segment:
                    # This is our current join clause, so the last table name we found
                    # is the source table
                    if table_names:
                        return table_names[-1]
                else:
                    # Previous join clause, add its table to our list
                    join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(join_clause)
                    if join_table_name:
                        table_names.append(join_table_name)
                        
        return None

    def _check_equality_ordering(self, on_condition, source_table: str, join_table: str) -> List:
        """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
        violations = []
        
        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
        for expr in expressions:
            # Look for comparison operators (=)
            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
            if not comparison_ops:
                continue
                
            # Get the left and right sides of the comparison
            children = expr.segments
            equals_op_index = None
            for i, child in enumerate(children):
                if hasattr(child, 'get_type') and child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
                    equals_op_index = i
                    break
                    
            if equals_op_index is None:
                continue
                
            # Get left and right operands
            if equals_op_index > 0 and equals_op_index < len(children) - 1:
                left_operand = children[equals_op_index - 1]
                right_operand = children[equals_op_index + 1]
                
                # Check if both operands are column references
                left_columns = list(left_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                right_columns = list(right_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                
                if left_columns and right_columns:
                    # Extract table names from column references
                    left_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(left_columns[0])
                    right_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(right_columns[0])
                    
                    # Debug info
                    # print(f"Comparing: {left_table} = {right_table}")
                    
                    # Check if this is a join between source and join table
                    if (left_table and right_table and 
                        ((left_table == source_table and right_table == join_table) or
                         (left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table))):
                        
                        # Violation if join table is on the left and source table is on the right
                        if left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table:
                            violations.append(expr)
                            
        return violations

    def _extract_table_name_from_column(self, column_ref) -> Optional[str]:
        """Extract table name from a column reference."""
        identifiers = list(column_ref.recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
        if len(identifiers) >= 2:
            # Format: table.column
            return identifiers[0].raw.lower()
        elif len(identifiers) == 1:
            # Could be just column name without table prefix
            # In this case we can't determine the table definitively
            pass
        return None</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py has been edited.      7	class Rule_CV12(BaseRule):
     8	    """In ``ON`` clause of ``JOIN`` statement, specify source table first and join table second.
     9	
    10	    **Anti-pattern**
    11	
    12	    In the ``ON`` clause, the join table is specified before the source table.
    13	
    14	    .. code-block:: sql
    15	       :force:
    16	
    17	        SELECT
    18	            source.col1,
    19	            other_source.col2
    20	        FROM source
    21	        LEFT JOIN other_source
    22	            ON other_source.id = source.id;
    23	
    24	    **Best practice**
    25	
    26	    In the ``ON`` clause, specify the source table before the join table.
    27	
    28	    .. code-block:: sql
    29	       :force:
    30	
    31	        SELECT
    32	            source.col1,
    33	            other_source.col2
    34	        FROM source
    35	        LEFT JOIN other_source
    36	            ON source.id = other_source.id;
    37	    """
    38

        """Check JOIN ON clause table ordering."""
        # We are only interested in JOIN clauses.
        assert context.segment.is_type("join_clause")

        # Simple test - just return a violation for any JOIN to see if the rule is working
        # return LintResult(description="Test violation")
        
        # Find the join table name (the table being joined)
        join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(context.segment)
        # print(f"Join table name: {join_table_name}")
        if not join_table_name:
            return None

        # Find the ON condition
        on_conditions = list(context.segment.recursive_crawl("join_on_condition"))
        # print(f"ON conditions: {len(on_conditions)}")
        if not on_conditions:
            return None

        on_condition = on_conditions[0]
        
        # Get the parent FROM clause to determine the source table
        source_table_name = self._get_source_table_name(context)
        # print(f"Source table name: {source_table_name}")
        if not source_table_name:
            return None

        # Check the ordering in equality expressions within the ON condition
        violations = self._check_equality_ordering(on_condition, source_table_name, join_table_name)
        # print(f"Violations: {len(violations)}")
        
        if violations:
            # Return the first violation
            return LintResult(
                anchor=violations[0],
                description=f"In join ON clause, source table '{source_table_name}' should appear before join table '{join_table_name}'."
            )

"""Detailed debug of the CV12 rule."""

import sqlfluff
from sqlfluff.core.rules import RuleContext
from sqlfluff.core.rules.config import FluffConfig

def debug_rule_detailed():
    """Debug the CV12 rule in detail."""
    
    # Test with the bad case that should fail
    sql = """SELECT *
FROM source
LEFT JOIN other_source
  ON other_source.id = source.id;"""
    
    print("=== Detailed Rule Debug ===")
    print(f"SQL: {sql}")
    
    # Parse and lint
    result = sqlfluff.lint(sql, dialect='ansi', rules=['CV12'])
    print(f"Linter result: {result}")

    # Try manual processing
    parsed = sqlfluff.parse(sql, dialect='ansi')
    if hasattr(parsed, 'tree') and parsed.tree:
        print("Found parsed tree")
        # Find join clauses
        join_clauses = list(parsed.tree.recursive_crawl("join_clause"))
        print(f"Found {len(join_clauses)} join clauses")
        
        for i, join_clause in enumerate(join_clauses):
            print(f"\nJoin clause {i}: {join_clause.raw}")
            
            # Import and instantiate the rule
            from sqlfluff.rules.convention.CV12 import Rule_CV12
            rule = Rule_CV12(config=FluffConfig())
            
            # Create a mock context (this is tricky)
            print("Attempting to call rule...")
            try:
                # This is difficult to do without the full context
                # Let's just test the helper methods
                join_table = rule._get_join_table_name(join_clause)
                print(f"Join table name: {join_table}")
                
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"Error calling rule methods: {e}")
                import traceback
                traceback.print_exc()

Let me add some prints directly to the rule file to see what's happening:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _eval(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[LintResult]:
        """Check JOIN ON clause table ordering."""
        # We are only interested in JOIN clauses.
        assert context.segment.is_type("join_clause")

        print(f"DEBUG: Processing join clause: {context.segment.raw}")
        
        # Simple test - just return a violation for any JOIN to see if the rule is working
        # return LintResult(description="Test violation")
        
        # Find the join table name (the table being joined)
        join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(context.segment)
        print(f"DEBUG: Join table name: {join_table_name}")
        if not join_table_name:
            print("DEBUG: No join table name found")
            return None

        # Find the ON condition
        on_conditions = list(context.segment.recursive_crawl("join_on_condition"))
        print(f"DEBUG: ON conditions: {len(on_conditions)}")
        if not on_conditions:
            print("DEBUG: No ON conditions found")
            return None

        on_condition = on_conditions[0]
        
        # Get the parent FROM clause to determine the source table
        source_table_name = self._get_source_table_name(context)
        print(f"DEBUG: Source table name: {source_table_name}")
        if not source_table_name:
            print("DEBUG: No source table name found")
            return None

        # Check the ordering in equality expressions within the ON condition
        violations = self._check_equality_ordering(on_condition, source_table_name, join_table_name)
        print(f"DEBUG: Violations: {len(violations)}")
        
        if violations:
            # Return the first violation
            return LintResult(
                anchor=violations[0],
                description=f"In join ON clause, source table '{source_table_name}' should appear before join table '{join_table_name}'."
            )

        return None</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _eval(self, context: RuleContext) -> Optional[LintResult]:
        """Check JOIN ON clause table ordering."""
        # We are only interested in JOIN clauses.
        assert context.segment.is_type("join_clause")

        # Simple test - just return a violation for any JOIN to see if the rule is working
        # return LintResult(description="Test violation")
        
        # Find the join table name (the table being joined)
        join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(context.segment)
        # print(f"Join table name: {join_table_name}")
        if not join_table_name:
            return None

        # Find the ON condition
        on_conditions = list(context.segment.recursive_crawl("join_on_condition"))
        # print(f"ON conditions: {len(on_conditions)}")
        if not on_conditions:
            return None

        on_condition = on_conditions[0]
        
        # Get the parent FROM clause to determine the source table
        source_table_name = self._get_source_table_name(context)
        # print(f"Source table name: {source_table_name}")
        if not source_table_name:
            return None

        # Check the ordering in equality expressions within the ON condition
        violations = self._check_equality_ordering(on_condition, source_table_name, join_table_name)
        # print(f"Violations: {len(violations)}")
        
        if violations:
            # Return the first violation
            return LintResult(
                anchor=violations[0],
                description=f"In join ON clause, source table '{source_table_name}' should appear before join table '{join_table_name}'."
            )

        return None</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py has been edited.
