        """Get the source table name for this join (previous table in FROM clause)."""
        # Walk up the parent stack to find the FROM clause
        parent_stack = list(context.parent_stack)
        
        # Find the containing select statement
        select_statement = None
        for parent in reversed(parent_stack):
            if parent.get_type() == "select_statement":
                select_statement = parent
                break
                
        if not select_statement:
            return None
            
        # Get all from expression elements to understand table order
        from_expressions = list(select_statement.recursive_crawl("from_expression"))
        if not from_expressions:
            return None
            
        # Get all table references in order
        table_names = []
        for from_expr in from_expressions:
            # Get direct table references (FROM table)
            from_elements = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("from_expression_element"))
            for element in from_elements:
                table_refs = list(element.recursive_crawl("table_reference"))
                for table_ref in table_refs:
                    identifiers = list(table_ref.recursive_crawl("naked_identifier"))
                    if identifiers:
                        table_names.append(identifiers[0].raw.lower())
            
            # Get join table references  
            join_clauses = list(from_expr.recursive_crawl("join_clause"))
            for join_clause in join_clauses:
                if join_clause is context.segment:
                    # This is our current join clause, so the last table name we found
                    # is the source table
                    if table_names:
                        return table_names[-1]
                else:
                    # Previous join clause, add its table to our list
                    join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(join_clause)
                    if join_table_name:
                        table_names.append(join_table_name)
                        
        return None</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py has been edited. Let me add more debug prints there:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _check_equality_ordering(self, on_condition, source_table: str, join_table: str) -> List:
        """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
        violations = []
        
        print(f"DEBUG: Checking equality ordering - source: {source_table}, join: {join_table}")
        
        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
        print(f"DEBUG: Found {len(expressions)} expressions")
        for i, expr in enumerate(expressions):
            print(f"DEBUG: Expression {i}: {expr.raw}")
            
            # Look for comparison operators (=)
            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
            print(f"DEBUG: Comparison ops in expr {i}: {len(comparison_ops)}")
            if not comparison_ops:
                continue
                
            # Get the left and right sides of the comparison
            children = expr.segments if hasattr(expr, 'segments') else []
            print(f"DEBUG: Expression {i} has {len(children)} children")
            equals_op_index = None
            for j, child in enumerate(children):
                if hasattr(child, 'get_type') and child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
                    equals_op_index = j
                    print(f"DEBUG: Found equals operator at index {j}: {child.raw}")
                    break
                    
            if equals_op_index is None:
                continue
                
            # Get left and right operands
            if equals_op_index > 0 and equals_op_index < len(children) - 1:
                left_operand = children[equals_op_index - 1]
                right_operand = children[equals_op_index + 1]
                print(f"DEBUG: Left operand: {left_operand.raw}")
                print(f"DEBUG: Right operand: {right_operand.raw}")
                
                # Check if both operands are column references
                left_columns = list(left_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                right_columns = list(right_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                print(f"DEBUG: Left columns: {len(left_columns)}")
                print(f"DEBUG: Right columns: {len(right_columns)}")
                
                if left_columns and right_columns:
                    # Extract table names from column references
                    left_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(left_columns[0])
                    right_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(right_columns[0])

                    print(f"DEBUG: Right table: {right_table}")

        """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
        violations = []
        
        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
        for expr in expressions:
            # Look for comparison operators (=)
            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
            if not comparison_ops:
                continue
                
            # Get the left and right sides of the comparison
            children = expr.segments if hasattr(expr, 'segments') else []
            equals_op_index = None
            for i, child in enumerate(children):
                if hasattr(child, 'get_type') and child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
                    equals_op_index = i
                    break
                    
            if equals_op_index is None:
                continue
                
            # Get left and right operands
            if equals_op_index > 0 and equals_op_index < len(children) - 1:
                left_operand = children[equals_op_index - 1]
                right_operand = children[equals_op_index + 1]
                
                # Check if both operands are column references
                left_columns = list(left_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                right_columns = list(right_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                
                if left_columns and right_columns:
                    # Extract table names from column references
                    left_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(left_columns[0])
                    right_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(right_columns[0])
                    
                    # Check if this is a join between source and join table
                    if (left_table and right_table and 
                        ((left_table == source_table and right_table == join_table) or
                         (left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table))):
                        
                        # Violation if join table is on the left and source table is on the right
                        if left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table:
                            violations.append(expr)
                            
        return violations</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py has been edited. [L:  4, P: 30]      |                                    dot:                      '.'
```
[L:  4, P: 31]      |                                    naked_identifier:         'id'
```

So the structure is:
1. column_reference (other_source.id)
2. whitespace
3. comparison_operator (=)
4. whitespace  
5. column_reference (source.id)

But I'm looking at the wrong level. The expression itself contains these children, not the operands as separate segments.         """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
        violations = []
        
        print(f"DEBUG: Checking equality ordering - source: {source_table}, join: {join_table}")
        
        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
        print(f"DEBUG: Found {len(expressions)} expressions")
        for i, expr in enumerate(expressions):
            print(f"DEBUG: Expression {i}: {expr.raw}")
            
            # Look for comparison operators (=) directly in this expression
            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
            print(f"DEBUG: Comparison ops in expr {i}: {len(comparison_ops)}")
            if not comparison_ops:
                continue
                
            # Get all the segments in this expression
            children = expr.segments if hasattr(expr, 'segments') else []
            print(f"DEBUG: Expression {i} has {len(children)} children")
            
            # Look for the position of the equals operator
            equals_positions = []
            for j, child in enumerate(children):
                if hasattr(child, 'get_type') and child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
                    equals_positions.append(j)
                    print(f"DEBUG: Found equals operator at index {j}: {child.raw}")
                    
            # Process each equals operator (there might be multiple in complex expressions)
            for equals_op_index in equals_positions:
                # Get left and right operands by looking at segments adjacent to the operator
                if equals_op_index > 0 and equals_op_index < len(children) - 1:
                    left_operand = children[equals_op_index - 1]
                    right_operand = children[equals_op_index + 1]
                    print(f"DEBUG: Left operand type: {left_operand.get_type()}, raw: '{left_operand.raw.strip()}'")
                    print(f"DEBUG: Right operand type: {right_operand.get_type()}, raw: '{right_operand.raw.strip()}'")

                    left_columns = list(left_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                    right_columns = list(right_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                    print(f"DEBUG: Left columns: {len(left_columns)}")
                    print(f"DEBUG: Right columns: {len(right_columns)}")

                        # Extract table names from column references
                        left_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(left_columns[0])
                        right_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(right_columns[0])

                        print(f"DEBUG: Right table: {right_table}")

        """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
        violations = []
        
        print(f"DEBUG: Checking equality ordering - source: {source_table}, join: {join_table}")
        
        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
        print(f"DEBUG: Found {len(expressions)} expressions")
        for i, expr in enumerate(expressions):
            print(f"DEBUG: Expression {i}: {expr.raw}")
            
            # Look for comparison operators (=) directly in this expression
            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
            print(f"DEBUG: Comparison ops in expr {i}: {len(comparison_ops)}")
            if not comparison_ops:
                continue
                
            # Get all the segments in this expression
            children = expr.segments if hasattr(expr, 'segments') else []
            print(f"DEBUG: Expression {i} has {len(children)} children")
            
            # Look for column references on both sides of comparison operators
            column_refs = list(expr.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
            print(f"DEBUG: Total column refs in expr {i}: {len(column_refs)}")
            
            # Look for the position of equals operators
            for j, child in enumerate(children):
                if hasattr(child, 'get_type') and child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
                    print(f"DEBUG: Found equals operator at index {j}: {child.raw}")
                    
                    # Find column references before and after this operator
                    left_column = None
                    right_column = None
                    
                    # Look backwards for the nearest column reference
                    for k in range(j - 1, -1, -1):
                        if children[k].get_type() == "column_reference":
                            left_column = children[k]
                            break
                            
                    # Look forwards for the nearest column reference  
                    for k in range(j + 1, len(children)):
                        if children[k].get_type() == "column_reference":
                            right_column = children[k]
                            break

                        print(f"DEBUG: Left column: {left_column.raw}")
                        print(f"DEBUG: Right column: {right_column.raw}")

                        right_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(right_column)

                        print(f"DEBUG: Right table: {right_table}")

        """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
        violations = []
        
        print(f"DEBUG: Checking equality ordering - source: {source_table}, join: {join_table}")
        
        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
        print(f"DEBUG: Found {len(expressions)} expressions")
        for i, expr in enumerate(expressions):
            print(f"DEBUG: Expression {i}: {expr.raw}")
            
            # Look for comparison operators (=) directly in this expression
            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
            print(f"DEBUG: Comparison ops in expr {i}: {len(comparison_ops)}")
            if not comparison_ops:
                continue
                
            # Get all the segments in this expression
            children = expr.segments if hasattr(expr, 'segments') else []
            print(f"DEBUG: Expression {i} has {len(children)} children")
            
            # Look for the position of the equals operator
            equals_positions = []
            for j, child in enumerate(children):
                if hasattr(child, 'get_type') and child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
                    equals_positions.append(j)
                    print(f"DEBUG: Found equals operator at index {j}: {child.raw}")
                    
            # Process each equals operator (there might be multiple in complex expressions)
            for equals_op_index in equals_positions:
                # Get left and right operands by looking at segments adjacent to the operator
                if equals_op_index > 0 and equals_op_index < len(children) - 1:
                    left_operand = children[equals_op_index - 1]
                    right_operand = children[equals_op_index + 1]
                    print(f"DEBUG: Left operand type: {left_operand.get_type()}, raw: '{left_operand.raw.strip()}'")
                    print(f"DEBUG: Right operand type: {right_operand.get_type()}, raw: '{right_operand.raw.strip()}'")
                    
                    # Check if both operands are column references or contain column references
                    left_columns = list(left_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                    right_columns = list(right_operand.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
                    print(f"DEBUG: Left columns: {len(left_columns)}")
                    print(f"DEBUG: Right columns: {len(right_columns)}")
                    
                    if left_columns and right_columns:
                        # Extract table names from column references
                        left_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(left_columns[0])
                        right_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(right_columns[0])
                        print(f"DEBUG: Left table: {left_table}")
                        print(f"DEBUG: Right table: {right_table}")
                        
                        # Check if this is a join between source and join table
                        if (left_table and right_table and 
                            ((left_table == source_table and right_table == join_table) or

                            # Violation if join table is on the left and source table is on the right
                            if left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table:
                                print(f"DEBUG: Found violation!")
                                violations.append(expr)
                            
        return violations</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py has been edited. Now let me test it with the good case to make sure it doesn't flag that:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/debug_rule_detailed.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Detailed debug of the CV12 rule."""

import sqlfluff

def debug_rule_detailed():
    """Debug the CV12 rule in detail."""
    
    # Test with the bad case that should fail
    bad_sql = """SELECT *
FROM source
LEFT JOIN other_source
  ON other_source.id = source.id;"""
    
    # Test with the good case that should pass
    good_sql = """SELECT *
FROM source
LEFT JOIN other_source
  ON source.id = other_source.id;"""
    
    print("=== Testing Bad Case (Should Fail) ===")
    print(f"SQL: {bad_sql}")
    result = sqlfluff.lint(bad_sql, dialect='ansi', rules=['CV12'])
    print(f"Linter result: {result}")

        """Check JOIN ON clause table ordering."""
        # We are only interested in JOIN clauses.
        assert context.segment.is_type("join_clause")

        # Find the join table name (the table being joined)
        join_table_name = self._get_join_table_name(context.segment)
        if not join_table_name:
            return None

        # Find the ON condition
        on_conditions = list(context.segment.recursive_crawl("join_on_condition"))
        if not on_conditions:
            return None

        on_condition = on_conditions[0]
        
        # Get the parent FROM clause to determine the source table
        source_table_name = self._get_source_table_name(context)
        if not source_table_name:
            return None

            return LintResult(

                description=f"In join ON clause, source table '{source_table_name}' should appear before join table '{join_table_name}'."

Edit the file again if necessary.</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/sqlfluff__sqlfluff__2.2/src/sqlfluff/rules/convention/CV12.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _check_equality_ordering(self, on_condition, source_table: str, join_table: str) -> List:
        """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
        violations = []
        
        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
        for expr in expressions:
            # Look for comparison operators (=) directly in this expression
            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
            if not comparison_ops:
                continue
                
            # Get all the segments in this expression
            children = expr.segments if hasattr(expr, 'segments') else []
            
            # Look for column references on both sides of comparison operators
            column_refs = list(expr.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
            
            # Look for the position of equals operators
            for j, child in enumerate(children):
                if hasattr(child, 'get_type') and child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
                    # Find column references before and after this operator
                    left_column = None
                    right_column = None
                    
                    # Look backwards for the nearest column reference
                    for k in range(j - 1, -1, -1):
                        if children[k].get_type() == "column_reference":
                            left_column = children[k]
                            break
                            
                    # Look forwards for the nearest column reference  
                    for k in range(j + 1, len(children)):
                        if children[k].get_type() == "column_reference":
                            right_column = children[k]
                            break
                            
                    if left_column and right_column:
                        # Extract table names from column references
                        left_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(left_column)
                        right_table = self._extract_table_name_from_column(right_column)
                        
                        # Check if this is a join between source and join table
                        if (left_table and right_table and 
                            ((left_table == source_table and right_table == join_table) or
                             (left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table))):
                            
                            # Violation if join table is on the left and source table is on the right
                            if left_table == join_table and right_table == source_table:
                                violations.append(expr)
                            
        return violations</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    def _check_equality_ordering(self, on_condition, source_table: str, join_table: str) -> List:
        """Check if equality expressions in ON clause have proper table ordering."""
        violations = []
        
        print(f"DEBUG: Checking equality ordering - source: {source_table}, join: {join_table}")
        
        # Look for equality expressions in the ON condition
        expressions = list(on_condition.recursive_crawl("expression"))
        print(f"DEBUG: Found {len(expressions)} expressions")
        for i, expr in enumerate(expressions):
            print(f"DEBUG: Expression {i}: {expr.raw}")
            
            # Look for comparison operators (=) directly in this expression
            comparison_ops = list(expr.recursive_crawl("comparison_operator"))
            print(f"DEBUG: Comparison ops in expr {i}: {len(comparison_ops)}")
            if not comparison_ops:
                continue
                
            # Get all the segments in this expression
            children = expr.segments if hasattr(expr, 'segments') else []
            print(f"DEBUG: Expression {i} has {len(children)} children")
            
            # Look for column references on both sides of comparison operators
            column_refs = list(expr.recursive_crawl("column_reference"))
            print(f"DEBUG: Total column refs in expr {i}: {len(column_refs)}")

            for j, child in enumerate(children):
                if hasattr(child, 'get_type') and child.get_type() == "comparison_operator" and "=" in child.raw:
